# Fault log

Every fault gets: root cause, a fix that stops it coming back, an undo, and a
check in `pdc_ops.fault_regression_check()` that runs daily.
Times are AWST (UTC+8). Staging Supabase `cdsmnqxtyyoeoznmbidd` only.
No customer names; key and stock numbers only.

Daily check: `select * from pdc_ops.fault_regression_check();` (service role or
PDC administrator). Any row with `ok = false` needs attention.

To add a check: insert a row into `pdc_ops.regression_checks` (as postgres, in
a migration) whose `check_sql` returns one row `(ok boolean, detail text)`. The
runner is STABLE, so a check cannot write. A release that changes a pinned
function must update `pdc_ops.expected_function_md5` in the same migration.

Key 415 and stock 13094088's November chain had been sliding one minute every minute | A started or stoppage booking whose planned end is already in the past is a cascade seed every minute, even when its effective end is still at or before that planned end. The tick sets the projected end to the next minute, walks the watermark, and re-saves every planned successor on the same bay, vehicle or technician. Each save bumps the row version. The Schedule path then sees the bay window as taken. A drag sends the old version to `administrator_move_workshop_booking`, which returns `version_conflict` before it looks at the window. The tick also takes the mutation lock and plans that component every minute, which is why a tick can take several seconds. Key 364 was the job Craig had just started; the chain still sliding later is behind running jobs whose planned end is already past (Key 235 on Fitting Bay 07, Key 251 on Accessory Fitting Bus4x4 Bay 10).
